"""
Subtitle Studio v3.0 — US/UK dictionary loader and converter.
CSV format: uk_word,us_word (one pair per line, no header).
"""

import csv
import os
import re


class Dictionary:
    """Loads a US/UK word pair CSV and converts text in either direction."""

    def __init__(self, csv_path: str):
        self.csv_path = csv_path
        self.uk_to_us: dict[str, str] = {}
        self.us_to_uk: dict[str, str] = {}
        self.loaded = False
        self._load()

    def _load(self):
        """Load CSV into both lookup dicts."""
        if not os.path.isfile(self.csv_path):
            return

        with open(self.csv_path, "r", encoding="utf-8-sig") as f:
            reader = csv.reader(f)
            for row in reader:
                if len(row) < 2:
                    continue
                uk = row[0].strip().lower()
                us = row[1].strip().lower()
                if uk and us:
                    self.uk_to_us[uk] = us
                    self.us_to_uk[us] = uk

        self.loaded = len(self.uk_to_us) > 0

    @property
    def pair_count(self) -> int:
        return len(self.uk_to_us)

    def contains_word(self, word: str) -> bool:
        """Return True if word exists in either side of the dictionary."""
        if not word:
            return False

        normalized = word.strip().lower()
        if not normalized:
            return False

        return normalized in self.uk_to_us or normalized in self.us_to_uk

    def convert_text(self, text: str, direction: str) -> tuple[str, int]:
        """
        Convert text using dictionary.

        Args:
            text: Input text
            direction: "uk_to_us" or "us_to_uk"

        Returns:
            (converted_text, change_count)
        """
        lookup = self.uk_to_us if direction == "uk_to_us" else self.us_to_uk
        change_count = 0

        def _replace_word(match):
            nonlocal change_count
            word = match.group(0)
            lower = word.lower()
            if lower in lookup:
                replacement = lookup[lower]
                # Preserve original case
                replacement = _match_case(word, replacement)
                change_count += 1
                return replacement
            return word

        # Match whole words only
        converted = re.sub(r"\b[a-zA-Z]+\b", _replace_word, text)
        return converted, change_count

    def preview_changes(self, texts: list[str], direction: str) -> list[dict]:
        """
        Scan a list of texts and return preview of all changes.

        Returns list of dicts:
            {index, original_word, converted_word, context}
        """
        lookup = self.uk_to_us if direction == "uk_to_us" else self.us_to_uk
        changes = []

        for idx, text in enumerate(texts):
            words = re.findall(r"\b[a-zA-Z]+\b", text)
            for word in words:
                lower = word.lower()
                if lower in lookup:
                    changes.append({
                        "index": idx,
                        "original_word": word,
                        "converted_word": _match_case(word, lookup[lower]),
                        "context": text[:80]
                    })

        return changes


def _match_case(original: str, replacement: str) -> str:
    """
    Preserve the case pattern of the original word.
    'Colour' -> 'Color', 'COLOUR' -> 'COLOR', 'colour' -> 'color'
    """
    if original.isupper():
        return replacement.upper()
    elif original[0].isupper():
        return replacement[0].upper() + replacement[1:]
    else:
        return replacement.lower()

# core/ffmpeg.py  — Subtitle Studio v4.0 Session 2
# FFmpeg / FFprobe wrappers with progress parsing

import os
import subprocess
import time
import re
import json
import platform

_SUBPROCESS_FLAGS = {}
if platform.system() == "Windows":
    _SUBPROCESS_FLAGS["creationflags"] = (
        getattr(subprocess, 'CREATE_NO_WINDOW', 0))


def probe_video(ffprobe_path, video_path):
    """
    Probe a video file and return metadata dict.
    Returns: duration, width, height, fps, codec,
             audio_codec, size_mb
    """
    file_name = os.path.basename(video_path) if video_path else "unknown file"

    cmd = [
        ffprobe_path,
        "-v", "error",
        "-print_format", "json",
        "-show_format",
        "-show_streams",
        video_path,
    ]

    try:
        result = subprocess.run(
            cmd,
            capture_output=True,
            text=True,
            timeout=30,
            creationflags=getattr(subprocess, "CREATE_NO_WINDOW", 0),
        )
    except FileNotFoundError:
        raise RuntimeError(f"ffprobe not found at: {ffprobe_path}")
    except subprocess.TimeoutExpired:
        raise RuntimeError(f"ffprobe timed out while inspecting: {file_name}")
    except Exception as e:
        raise RuntimeError(f"ffprobe failed while inspecting {file_name}: {e}")

    if result.returncode != 0:
        stderr = (result.stderr or "").strip()
        stdout = (result.stdout or "").strip()

        details = stderr or stdout or "ffprobe returned no error details."
        details = details[-600:]

        raise RuntimeError(
            "Could not inspect video. The file may be corrupt, incomplete, "
            f"or unsupported.\nFile: {file_name}\n"
            f"ffprobe exit code: {result.returncode}\n"
            f"Details: {details}"
        )

    stdout = (result.stdout or "").strip()
    if not stdout:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Could not inspect video. ffprobe returned no metadata.\n"
            f"File: {file_name}"
        )

    try:
        data = json.loads(stdout)
    except json.JSONDecodeError as e:
        sample = stdout[:600] if stdout else "empty output"
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Could not inspect video. ffprobe returned invalid metadata.\n"
            f"File: {file_name}\n"
            f"Details: {e}\n"
            f"Output: {sample}"
        )

    # Find video and audio streams
    video_stream = None
    audio_stream = None

    for stream in data.get("streams", []):
        if stream.get("codec_type") == "video" and video_stream is None:
            video_stream = stream
        if stream.get("codec_type") == "audio" and audio_stream is None:
            audio_stream = stream

    if not video_stream:
        raise RuntimeError(
            "Could not inspect video. No video stream was found.\n"
            f"File: {file_name}"
        )

    fmt = data.get("format", {})

    # Duration
    try:
        duration = float(video_stream.get("duration", fmt.get("duration", 0)) or 0)
    except Exception:
        duration = 0.0

    # FPS
    fps_str = video_stream.get("r_frame_rate", "30/1")
    try:
        num, den = fps_str.split("/")
        den_f = float(den)
        fps = round(float(num) / den_f, 2) if den_f else 30.0
    except Exception:
        fps = 30.0

    # File size
    try:
        size_bytes = int(fmt.get("size", 0) or 0)
        size_mb = round(size_bytes / (1024 * 1024), 1)
    except Exception:
        size_mb = 0

    return {
        "duration": duration,
        "width": int(video_stream.get("width", 0) or 0),
        "height": int(video_stream.get("height", 0) or 0),
        "fps": fps,
        "codec": video_stream.get("codec_name", "unknown"),
        "audio_codec": (
            audio_stream.get("codec_name", "none")
            if audio_stream else "none"
        ),
        "size_mb": size_mb,
    }


def run_ffmpeg(ffmpeg_path, args, log_fn,
               progress_fn=None, duration=None,
               cancel_event=None, process_holder=None):
    """
    Run an FFmpeg command with progress parsing.

    To avoid UI freezes, FFmpeg stderr is not fully echoed line-by-line.
    We only emit:
    - startup summary
    - occasional progress status
    - final success/failure
    - last meaningful error line on failure
    """
    cmd = [ffmpeg_path] + args
    log_fn(f"Running: {' '.join(cmd[:6])}...")

    start_time = time.time()
    last_progress_log = 0.0
    last_error_line = ""
    time_pattern = re.compile(
        r"time=(\d{2}):(\d{2}):(\d{2})\.(\d{2,3})")

    try:
        proc = subprocess.Popen(
            cmd,
            stdout=subprocess.PIPE,
            stderr=subprocess.PIPE,
            universal_newlines=True,
            creationflags=getattr(
                subprocess, 'CREATE_NO_WINDOW', 0))

        if process_holder is not None:
            process_holder["process"] = proc

        for raw_line in proc.stderr:
            if cancel_event is not None and cancel_event.is_set():
                try:
                    proc.terminate()
                except Exception:
                    pass

                try:
                    proc.wait(timeout=5)
                except Exception:
                    try:
                        proc.kill()
                    except Exception:
                        pass

                log_fn("FFmpeg cancelled.")
                if progress_fn:
                    progress_fn(0, "Cancelled")
                return False

            line = raw_line.strip()
            if not line:
                continue

            # Keep last potentially useful error/status line
            lowered = line.lower()
            if ("error" in lowered
                    or "invalid" in lowered
                    or "failed" in lowered
                    or "unable" in lowered
                    or "no such file" in lowered):
                last_error_line = line

            # Parse progress without flooding the UI log
            if progress_fn and duration and duration > 0:
                match = time_pattern.search(line)
                if match:
                    h, m, s, frac = match.groups()
                    if len(frac) == 2:
                        frac = frac + "0"

                    current = (
                        int(h) * 3600
                        + int(m) * 60
                        + int(s)
                        + int(frac) / 1000.0
                    )

                    percent = min(
                        current / duration * 100.0,
                        99.9)

                    elapsed = time.time() - start_time
                    if percent > 0.5:
                        total_est = elapsed / (percent / 100.0)
                        remaining = max(total_est - elapsed, 0)
                        if remaining >= 60:
                            mins = int(remaining // 60)
                            secs = int(remaining % 60)
                            eta_str = f"{mins}m {secs}s remaining"
                        else:
                            eta_str = f"{int(remaining)}s remaining"
                    else:
                        eta_str = "Calculating..."

                    progress_fn(percent, eta_str)

                    # Log only every ~10% to avoid queue flooding
                    if percent - last_progress_log >= 10:
                        log_fn(f"Export progress: {percent:.0f}%")
                        last_progress_log = percent

        proc.wait()

        if process_holder is not None:
            process_holder["process"] = None

        if proc.returncode == 0:
            if progress_fn:
                progress_fn(100.0, "Complete")
            log_fn("FFmpeg finished successfully.")
            return True

        if last_error_line:
            log_fn(f"FFmpeg error: {last_error_line}")
        log_fn(f"FFmpeg exited with code {proc.returncode}")
        if progress_fn:
            progress_fn(0, "Failed")
        return False

    except FileNotFoundError:
        if process_holder is not None:
            process_holder["process"] = None
        log_fn(f"FFmpeg not found at: {ffmpeg_path}")
        return False
    except Exception as e:
        if process_holder is not None:
            process_holder["process"] = None
        log_fn(f"FFmpeg error: {e}")
        return False

FILE: core/parser.py

"""
Subtitle Studio v3.0 — SRT/VTT parser and writer.
Parses subtitle files into Cue objects. Writes them back to any format.
"""

import re
from core.state import Cue


# ── Time conversion ────────────────────────────────────────

def time_to_seconds(time_str: str) -> float:
    """
    Convert timecode string to seconds.
    Handles:  HH:MM:SS,mmm  (SRT)
              HH:MM:SS.mmm  (VTT)
              MM:SS.mmm     (VTT short form)
    """
    time_str = time_str.strip().replace(",", ".")

    # HH:MM:SS.mmm
    m = re.match(r"(\d+):(\d+):(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?", time_str)
    if m:
        h, mi, s = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2)), int(m.group(3))
        ms = int(m.group(4).ljust(3, "0")[:3]) if m.group(4) else 0
        return h * 3600 + mi * 60 + s + ms / 1000.0

    # MM:SS.mmm (no hours)
    m = re.match(r"(\d+):(\d+)(?:\.(\d+))?", time_str)
    if m:
        mi, s = int(m.group(1)), int(m.group(2))
        ms = int(m.group(3).ljust(3, "0")[:3]) if m.group(3) else 0
        return mi * 60 + s + ms / 1000.0

    return 0.0


def seconds_to_srt_time(seconds: float) -> str:
    """Convert seconds to SRT format: HH:MM:SS,mmm"""
    if seconds < 0:
        seconds = 0.0
    h = int(seconds // 3600)
    m = int((seconds % 3600) // 60)
    s = int(seconds % 60)
    ms = int(round((seconds - int(seconds)) * 1000))
    if ms >= 1000:
        ms = 999
    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}:{s:02d},{ms:03d}"


def seconds_to_vtt_time(seconds: float) -> str:
    """Convert seconds to VTT format: HH:MM:SS.mmm"""
    return seconds_to_srt_time(seconds).replace(",", ".")


def seconds_to_display(s: float) -> str:
    """Convert seconds to HH:MM:SS.mmm display format.

    Subtitle editing requires millisecond precision. This format is also
    accepted by time_to_seconds(), so displayed values can round-trip safely
    through edit fields without losing timing accuracy.
    """
    try:
        s = float(s)
    except Exception:
        s = 0.0

    if s < 0:
        s = 0.0

    total_ms = int(round(s * 1000))
    h = total_ms // 3_600_000
    total_ms %= 3_600_000
    m = total_ms // 60_000
    total_ms %= 60_000
    sec = total_ms // 1000
    ms = total_ms % 1000

    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}:{sec:02d}.{ms:03d}"


# ── Parsing ────────────────────────────────────────────────

def parse_subtitle_file(filepath: str) -> list[Cue]:
    """
    Parse an SRT or VTT file into a list of Cue objects.
    Auto-detects format by extension and content.
    Returns cues sorted by start time.
    """
    with open(filepath, "r", encoding="utf-8-sig") as f:
        content = f.read()

    # Detect format
    if filepath.lower().endswith(".vtt") or content.strip().startswith("WEBVTT"):
        return _parse_vtt(content)
    else:
        return _parse_srt(content)


def _parse_srt(content: str) -> list[Cue]:
    """Parse SRT content into Cue list."""
    cues = []
    # Split on blank lines to get blocks
    blocks = re.split(r"\n\s*\n", content.strip())

    for block in blocks:
        lines = block.strip().split("\n")
        if len(lines) < 2:
            continue

        # Find the timecode line (contains " --> ")
        time_line_idx = None
        for i, line in enumerate(lines):
            if " --> " in line:
                time_line_idx = i
                break

        if time_line_idx is None:
            continue

        # Parse timecodes
        time_parts = lines[time_line_idx].split(" --> ")
        if len(time_parts) != 2:
            continue

        start = time_to_seconds(time_parts[0])
        end = time_to_seconds(time_parts[1].split()[0])  # strip position tags

        # Text is everything after the timecode line
        text = "\n".join(lines[time_line_idx + 1:]).strip()
        if not text:
            continue

        cues.append(Cue(
            index=len(cues) + 1,
            start=start,
            end=end,
            text=text
        ))

    return cues


def _parse_vtt(content: str) -> list[Cue]:
    """Parse WebVTT content into Cue list."""
    cues = []
    # Remove WEBVTT header and NOTE blocks
    lines = content.split("\n")
    cleaned = []
    skip_block = False
    for line in lines:
        if line.strip().startswith("WEBVTT"):
            continue
        if line.strip().startswith("NOTE"):
            skip_block = True
            continue
        if skip_block and line.strip() == "":
            skip_block = False
            continue
        if skip_block:
            continue
        cleaned.append(line)

    # Now parse like SRT (VTT uses same block structure)
    blocks = re.split(r"\n\s*\n", "\n".join(cleaned).strip())

    for block in blocks:
        lines = block.strip().split("\n")
        if len(lines) < 2:
            # Could be a single timecode + text on one line
            if len(lines) == 1 and " --> " in lines[0]:
                continue  # timecode only, no text
            elif len(lines) < 1:
                continue

        # Find timecode line
        time_line_idx = None
        for i, line in enumerate(lines):
            if " --> " in line:
                time_line_idx = i
                break

        if time_line_idx is None:
            continue

        time_parts = lines[time_line_idx].split(" --> ")
        if len(time_parts) != 2:
            continue

        start = time_to_seconds(time_parts[0])
        end = time_to_seconds(time_parts[1].split()[0])

        text = "\n".join(lines[time_line_idx + 1:]).strip()
        # Strip VTT formatting tags like <b>, <i>, etc.
        text = re.sub(r"<[^>]+>", "", text)
        if not text:
            continue

        cues.append(Cue(
            index=len(cues) + 1,
            start=start,
            end=end,
            text=text
        ))

    return cues


# ── Writing ────────────────────────────────────────────────

def write_srt(cues: list[Cue], filepath: str):
    """Write cues to an SRT file."""
    with open(filepath, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for i, cue in enumerate(cues, 1):
            f.write(f"{i}\n")
            f.write(f"{seconds_to_srt_time(cue.start)} --> {seconds_to_srt_time(cue.end)}\n")
            f.write(f"{cue.text}\n\n")


def write_vtt(cues: list[Cue], filepath: str):
    """Write cues to a WebVTT file."""
    with open(filepath, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        f.write("WEBVTT\n\n")
        for i, cue in enumerate(cues, 1):
            f.write(f"{i}\n")
            f.write(f"{seconds_to_vtt_time(cue.start)} --> {seconds_to_vtt_time(cue.end)}\n")
            f.write(f"{cue.text}\n\n")


def write_txt(cues: list[Cue], filepath: str):
    """Write cues as plain text transcript (no timecodes)."""
    with open(filepath, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for cue in cues:
            f.write(f"{cue.text}\n")


def cues_to_srt_string(cues: list[Cue]) -> str:
    """Return SRT content as a string (for temp files during burn)."""
    lines = []
    for i, cue in enumerate(cues, 1):
        lines.append(f"{i}")
        lines.append(f"{seconds_to_srt_time(cue.start)} --> {seconds_to_srt_time(cue.end)}")
        lines.append(cue.text)
        lines.append("")
    return "\n".join(lines)

FILE: core/player.py
